# MS.990 Parallel Edition: Canvases 155–184

## Page 184 — IIIF Canvas #155

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0155.JP2` (6047 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0155.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0155.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,150 / 2,500

Page 184 (folio recto) presents recipes for ordinary fever drinks ('Waßer für das ordinari Trankh in Jeden fieber'), cooling drinks for feverish patients and thirst, a water substitute for wine, followed by a treatise on double tertian and continuous fevers ('Volgt Weider Von den Dopleten 3 taglichen auch Continuierlichen fiebern') with electuaries, laxative chicory vinegar, and purging/post-purging regimens. Illustrated with a shaded ink-and-wash pitcher/jug at the top of the left column.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Illustration 1 (Canvas 155)** (`#xywh=350,500,2373,1800`): A small shaded ink-and-wash drawing of a round-bellied jug/pitcher with a handle in the upper left margin beside the rubric 'Waßer für das ordinari Trankh in Jeden fieber'.

## Page 185 — IIIF Canvas #156

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0156.JP2` (5702 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0156.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0156.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,150 / 2,550

Page 185 (folio verso) continues with fever remedies in full-width lines across the upper two-thirds, covering quotidian, tertian, and stomach fevers (including roasted nutmeg, quenched iron in wine, asarabacca, coriander in a soft egg, and a sympathetic dough-and-dog remedy for children). The lower third features a framed colored illustration on the left of a wrapped elderly/sickly person leaning on a staff beside a door, captioned with critical and decretory days, paired on the right with a treatise explaining 'Dies Critici' and 'Dies Decretorii' (critical and decretory days in severe fevers, highlighting day 7 as the most dangerous).

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**dies Critici. 7. 14. 21. 27. / dies Decretorej. 4. 9. 14. 17. 21. 23. 27.** (`#xywh=850,500,2201,1800`): A framed colored ink-and-wash illustration in the lower left showing an elderly or sickly figure wearing a white headwrap, dark cloak with a yellow collar, and long reddish-brown skirt, leaning on a walking stick indoors beside a wooden door.

## Page 186 — IIIF Canvas #157

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0157.JP2` (5942 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0157.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0157.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,200 / 2,100

Continuation of medical treatise on critical days (dies critici and dies decretorii) and signs of concoction in fevers, followed by a major rubricated section on the causes and recognition of fevers (ephemera, synocha, putrida, and malignant continuous fevers).

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0158.JP2` (5673 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0158.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0158.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,200 / 2,300

Two-column page continuing the classification of continuous, double tertian, quartan, and hemitritaeus fevers, followed by a major section 'Von Etlichen Zeichen so sich bey den fiebern finden Was sye bedeiden' detailing diagnostic and prognostic signs in fevers (nosebleeds, deafness, chills on the 6th day, angina, sweating, sleep sweats, and continuous vs. intermittent fevers).

Continuation of fever treatment (cooling broth, mild laxative, leeches to the hemorrhoidal vein, cupping glasses, and senna/rhubarb infusion), followed by sections on symptoms of double continuous tertian fever ('febre Terzana dopio Continoe'), its cure ('Chur') including bloodletting, clysters, boluses, and emetics, and a section on sweating ('Von Schwizen in disen fieber').

Two-column page continuing remedies for excessive sweating (astringent oils, cooling air), followed by sections on continual vomiting ('Von Continuierlichen Bröchen') with recipes using quince juice, corals, and oxymel, a recipe section ('So braucht Volgente Mitel') for phlegm and melancholy vomiting, and a section on insomnia in fever ('Von lohrnen Schlaff, V über flisiges Wachen in fieber') with cooling diets, melon milk, and soporific foot-baths.

## Page 191 — IIIF Canvas #162

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0162.JP2` (5701 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0162.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0162.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,500 / 2,050

Two-column page continuing external and internal soporific remedies (opium/saffron ointment, poppy syrup, Philonium Romanum, Trifera magna), followed by a section on lethargy/sleeping sickness in fever ('Schlaff Sucht. in fieber') with clysters, suppositories ('Zäpfl.'), tickling the nose, shaving the head, and a colored drawing of a human head showing the coronal suture ('Commissura Coronale') for topical/cautery application, plus a precious syrup recipe.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Illustration of the coronal suture on the head (adjacent to 'Volgenter Sÿrup ist sehr khöstlich')** (`#xywh=350,500,2200,1800`): Pen-and-ink and watercolor wash drawing of a human head tilted slightly forward, with brown curly hair, downcast eyes, nose, and mouth. Red lines mark a horizontal band around the forehead/temples intersecting a vertical line along the sagittal suture at a small red circle on the coronal suture ('Commissura Coronale'), illustrating the site of application referenced in the text above.

## Page 192 — IIIF Canvas #163

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0163.JP2` (6021 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0163.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0163.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,800 / 1,650

Treatise on petechiae (spotted fever), classifying red, white/violet, and yellow/black spots by severity and prognosis, accompanied by a watercolor illustration of a spotted patient holding a rosary in bed attended by a female caregiver, followed by purgative, blistering, bezoar, Armenian bole, and cooling remedies, including treatments for pregnant women.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Illustration of a patient with petechiae in bed** (`#xywh=350,500,2360,1800`): Framed rectangular watercolor showing a bearded male patient propped up on white pillows in a wooden bed under a white sheet, bare-chested with red petechial spots on his torso and arms, wearing a red headband and holding a long black-and-gold rosary; to the right stands a female attendant in a purplish-pink dress and dark cap holding a shallow brown basin, with a table bearing a bowl of red fruit in the background.

## Page 193 — IIIF Canvas #164

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0164.JP2` (5653 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0164.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0164.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,800 / 1,850

Continuation of the petechial drink recipe, followed by sections on Febris Epiala (coldness predominating over heat), Febris Lipyria (internal burning with external coldness, symptoms, and cooling/bleeding treatment), and Febris Ardens (burning fever from yellow bile, mortal symptoms, and cooling/purgative cures).

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0165.JP2` (5997 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0165.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0165.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,800 / 1,750

Treatise on Febris Quartana (four-day fever), attributed to putrid melancholic bile and tough phlegm; the author criticizes slow conventional methods and presents his own successful multi-stage cure consisting of purgative powders, drinks, or electuaries, digestive pills, and an herbal broth.

## Page 195 — IIIF Canvas #166

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0166.JP2` (5699 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0166.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0166.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,600 / 2,800

Page 195 (verso) of Wellcome Collection MS.990 contains early modern German and Latin medical recipes for treating quartan and tertian fevers. The left column presents a complex electuary ('Die Lattwerung mach also') incorporating aromatic roots, herbs, hartshorn, coral, seeds, honey, and Peruvian bark (cinchona), followed by detailed German dosing and regimen instructions. The right column continues the regimen, warning against overindulgence after recovery, and adds four further fever remedies ('Volgen noch andere Mitl fir das 4 tägliche fieber', two entries titled 'Ein Anders', and 'Arcana fir das 4 tägliche vnd 3 tägliche fieber') using mithridatium, Peruvian bark, arcanum duplicatum, antimony, alchemical salts, laudanum, and herbal infusions. Two ink manicules appear on the page (one in the left column beside 'hirundinaria. Scorzonera. āā ℥j.' and one beneath the final red heading in the right column).

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0167.JP2` (5930 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0167.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0167.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,450

A 17th-century German and Latin medical recipe collection (Wellcome Collection MS.990, p. 396) arranged in two columns, containing remedies and purgative potions for quartan and continuous fevers, including herbal decoctions, emollient poultices for splenic pain, gentian, rose water, tobacco water, peppercorns, and a foot cataplasm.

## Page 97 — IIIF Canvas #168

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0168.JP2` (5671 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0168.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0168.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,200 / 1,450

Folio 97 verso, titled 'Von den Ungewissen fieber, oder Ungewissen kranckheit' (Of the uncertain fever, or uncertain illness), attributes epidemic or 'uncertain' fevers to divine punishment and miasmatic corruption of the air caused by unfavorable celestial constellations and planetary aspects. A large central watercolor illustration depicts a bare-chested patient sitting up in bed attended by a physician gesturing toward medicines and a crucifix on a table, while a weeping woman stands in the doorway on the left. The text below describes how poisoned air and crops transmit toxins into the body, producing diverse symptoms such as intense heat, inflammation, severe headache, unquenchable thirst, diarrhea, and loss of senses/appetite.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Bedside scene of a fever patient, physician, and grieving woman** (`#xywh=850,500,2185,1800`): A central watercolor illustration showing a bare-chested patient with wavy brown hair sitting propped up on white pillows in a wooden bed under a white sheet and mottled rose-red coverlet. To the right, a physician in a black skullcap, white collar, and fur-trimmed robe rests his right hand on the patient's shoulder while pointing with his left toward a wooden table beneath a four-paned window; on the table stand a small crucifix, apothecary jars and flasks (containing yellow and pink liquids), and a bowl with a spoon. On the far left, a woman in a red bodice and white skirt stands in an open doorway, holding a cloth to her face in grief.

## Page 398 — IIIF Canvas #169

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0169.JP2` (6004 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0169.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0169.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,600 / 2,850

A folio from a late 17th-century German medical manuscript (Wellcome MS.990, p. 398) discussing contagious fevers ('Contagios oder anfengig'). The text describes how pestilential poisons enter the body through air, food, drink, breath, stench, or fear, attacking the heart and brain. It provides preventive measures including fumigation with juniper wood, masterwort, and lovage, along with purgation, bloodletting, and several compound recipes (an electuary with theriac, Armenian bole, and alkermes; pills with myrrh, gum ammoniac, and aloes; and a 'famous poison powder' of valerian, swallowwort, polypody, lovage, angelica, and herb Paris). The final section addresses the cure of the fever, noting its severity as a divine punishment requiring repentance before natural remedies, and warning physicians that deceptive pulses and urine signs can mask the illness.

### Main text

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | oder zu vill hafftig, Naſſen blutten, großen fröſten, brächen vd groſſe onmachten geben vd groſſe | or too violently, [causing] nosebleeds, great chills, vomiting, and great fainting fits, and great *— Note: Continuation from p. 397; 'vd' is a standard abbreviation for 'und'.* |
| 2 | kranckheiten. Vnd nach dem das gifft geartet oder Natur hat. ſo erweckt es in ſolchem leib die ſchwere | illnesses. And according to the nature or disposition of the poison, it awakens in such a body the severe |
| 3 | zufäll. Dan glich wie allerley gifft auf ſich geſucht worden, ſo woll in der Erden als in denen thieren, | symptoms. For just as all manner of poisons have been sought out, both in the earth and in animals, |
| 4 | hat ain yedes ſein beſondere wirckung. alſö iſt die gifft die in vnſern leiber zeicht durch den lufft | each has its own particular effect; thus is the poison which draws into our bodies through the air |
| 5 | oder durch ſpeiſ vd tranck gemiſcht, nach Ihrer art vd aigenſchafft vnſer leiber angreifft vd heimſucht. | or mixed through food and drink, [which] according to its kind and property attacks and afflicts our bodies. |

| 8 | ſo ſie in vnſern leib kompt, zeicht durch das bluett Ihren weeg zum Herzen vd hirn, zu dodten die | when it enters our body, draws its way through the blood to the heart and brain, to kill the |
| 9 | lebens geiſter, doſern bey etlichen hizige bluett währe, mehrere gefar iſt, als bey andern. Jhr ſchware | vital spirits; insofar as some have hot blood, there is greater danger than with others. Their severe |

## Page f. 99. — IIIF Canvas #170

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0170.JP2` (5699 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0170.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0170.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,650 / 3,400

Folio 99 verso of Wellcome Collection MS.990 (c. 1675) continues a treatise on pestilential fever and its treatment. The left column outlines the fivefold (headed '4 weg') therapeutic regimen—air correction, vomitives, purgation via the bowels, sweating, and tempering of heat and cold—followed by diagnostic signs of acute thirst, precordial oppression, and shivering, and introduces a protective fumigation ('Rauch') accompanied by an illustration of a smoking censer. The right column provides the compound fumigation recipe (including olibanum, amber, myrrh, mastic, asa foetida, hartshorn, angelica, pimpernel, and spun hemp, silk, gold, and silver threads) and a section on therapeutic vomiting ('Yber brechen in disen fieber') illustrated by a man vomiting into a tub, recommending mild emetics such as linseed, walnut, or bitter almond oil, hazelwort with oxymel, and a feather dipped in scorpion oil.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Fumigation censer (bottom left)** (`#xywh=850,500,2199,1800`): A footed chalice-like censer or brazier resting on a yellow base, filled with glowing red-orange coals and herbs, with grey smoke billowing upward and a small ladle or pestle resting against the left side.

**Man vomiting into a tub (middle right)** (`#xywh=850,2300,2199,1800`): A bearded man in a grey tunic leaning forward over a round, banded wooden or ceramic tub, vomiting a stream of yellow-red bilious matter into the vessel while holding its rim.

## Page 208 — IIIF Canvas #171

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0171.JP2` (6020 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0171.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0171.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,500 / 2,850

Folio 208r of Wellcome MS.990 presents two columns of 17th-century German and Latin medical recipes and therapeutic instructions. The left column opens with a secret arcanum ('Ein schönes Arcana') prepared by distilling bryony root, cyclamen root, and white hellebore in water, administered in Malmsey wine via a small glass pipette (accompanied by an inline triangular symbol/sketch of the glass tip) to purge contagious poisons from the stomach, followed by a section attributed to 'H. Rönötten' on removing the cause of the illness through purgation, clysters, bloodletting, and especially sweating. The right column discusses sweating as a sure remedy for this and other fevers, warning against overheating ingredients (brandy, pepper, pellitory, castoreum, camphor, sulfur), debating whether camphor cools or heats, and providing several sudorific recipes (angelica root with theriac, elecampane in red wine, lovage root with wine vinegar, rue juice with theriac) as well as external warming methods using vinegar-moistened bricks, hot water flasks, and a vinegar-and-theriac compress applied over the navel.

## Page 201 — IIIF Canvas #172

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0172.JP2` (5702 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0172.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0172.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,400

Page 201 (verso) in two columns. The upper left continues a regimen on managing sweat and preventing sleep in poisoned or feverish patients (including heart-strengthening cordials). The main section 'Vom Aderlasen' (Of Bloodletting) argues against bloodletting in this continuous fever because it weakens the body's moisture and drives heat toward the heart, brain, chest, or kidneys, followed by a red rubric noting that experience shows all who died had been bled. The right column concludes with remarks on aiding natural purgation (vomiting, suppositories, or laxative foods).

## Page 202 — IIIF Canvas #173

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0173.JP2` (6014 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0173.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0173.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,300

Page 202 (recto) in two columns. Continues general remarks on fever heat, then presents 'Von der Hiz, und dero Küellung' (Of Heat and its Cooling), detailing cooling foods, waters, syrups (rose, violet, lemon, pomegranate, mulberry, elderberry, citron), and warning against applying cold poultices directly to the heart or stomach. The right column adds lukewarm ointments, cooling lotions (rosewater, nightshade, salt, rue), and introduces 'Von Allerley Zue fäll dises fiebers, als erstlich von der Tobsucht und haubt Wehe' (Of All Kinds of Symptoms of this Fever, Firstly Frenzy and Headache).

## Page 203 — IIIF Canvas #174

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0174.JP2` (5684 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0174.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0174.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,300

Page 203 (verso), written in a single full-page column. Continues distinguishing the symptoms of hot/bilious headache and lethargy, then presents 'Von den hitzigen bösenden Kopff Wehe' (Of the Hot Malignant Headache) with recipes for rosewater, salt, Armenian bole, barley milk, and—if delirium ensues—applying a freshly split black hen or warm calf/lamb lung to the shaved head. Concludes with 'Von khalten Schläfferigen Wehe tage des Haubts' (Of Cold Lethargic Headache) and a spiced vinegar decoction (angelica, masterwort, zedoary, tormentil, dittany, red roses, marjoram, lavender).

### Full Page

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | 203 | 203 *— Note: Page number in red box at top left.* |

## Page 204 — IIIF Canvas #175

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0175.JP2` (5935 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0175.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0175.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,800 / 2,400

Page 204 (recto) of Wellcome MS.990, arranged in two columns, continues remedies for sleeping sickness (Schlafsucht) including external plasters, vinegar-boiled herbs, and brain-raging treatments; followed by a major red-ink heading 'Von den Wirmen in diser khranckheit' (Of the worms in this sickness) detailing symptoms of parasitic worms, anthelmintic powders (rhubarb, aloe, myrrh, carline thistle, euphorbium, hartshorn, dittany, corallina), and a second major red-ink heading 'Von den Schwaissen' (Of the sweats) introducing critical vs. non-critical sweats.

## Page 205 — IIIF Canvas #176

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0176.JP2` (5692 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0176.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0176.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,800 / 2,500

Page 205 (verso), arranged in two columns, continues the treatise on sweats ('Von den Schwaissen'), distinguishing two types: non-decretory cold/clammy sweats (with warnings against featherbeds, instructions to prevent sleep by applying red/rose vinegar with lavender or rosewater to the nose and pulses) and critical sweats ('sudores critici') on the 3rd, 5th, or 7th day (to be wiped with warm cloths and checked with quince or myrtle oil if excessive). In the lower right column, a major red-ink heading 'Von Vergicht, Krampf, Omachten und sewer gliedrn' introduces convulsions, cramps, fainting, and heavy limbs.

## Page 206 — IIIF Canvas #177

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0177.JP2` (6022 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0177.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0177.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,800 / 2,300

Page 206 (recto), written in a single full-page column, concludes the chapter on convulsions and cramps with instructions to bind the patient with soft cloths, revive them with amber smoke, frankincense, angelica, and rub the limbs with cooling or warming oils (rose, violet, goose/duck fat vs. castoreum, chamomile, fox fat). A major red-ink heading 'Von Ver Liehrung Und Widerbringung der Khräfften' (Of the Loss and Restoration of Strengths) then discusses the three spirits (animalis, vitalis, naturalis) and prescribes nourishing broths, capon, chicken, veal, doves, alkermes, rosewater, lemon/citron juice, and aromatics.

## Page 207 — IIIF Canvas #178

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0178.JP2` (5717 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0178.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0178.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,800 / 2,400

Continuation of a treatise on acute/continuous fevers, covering cooling remedies for a heated liver, sudden swoonings (Von Jächlingen omachten) from cold, heat, or bleeding, heart palpitations (Von Gertz khlopffen), facial signs of impending death (Hippocratic facies), and cordial heart-poultices and smelling-bags (Volgen Von den herz Söckhsen).

Continuation of cordial poultice and aqua vitae recipes for swooning, followed by a new illustrated section on secondary facial, ear, gum, and cheek symptoms (Von andern Zuefällen als fallen sind an den ohren am Wang, augen vnd Zanfleisch vnd geschwulst des angesichts), featuring a watercolor bust portrait of a youth with a red cheek swelling, and remedies for cheek/jaw swellings, toothache, and ear discharge/deafness.

**Portrait of a youth with a cheek swelling** (`#xywh=850,500,2344,1800`): Framed watercolor bust portrait in the upper-middle left column depicting a young man with dark curly hair facing forward, showing a prominent, rounded, inflamed red swelling on his left cheek (malar/parotid region), illustrating the section on facial, ear, and cheek swellings.

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0180.JP2` (5738 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0180.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0180.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,500

Illustrated medical treatise sections covering Eye Pains (Augen schmerzen), with recipes for eye-drops and poultices; Nosebleeds (Von Nasen Fluetten), noting critical-day bleeding vs. dangerous hemorrhage; and Quinsy, Mouth, Tonsil, and Throat Ulcers (Von der Brein. Mundt Mandel vnd Hals geschwer), with gargle recipes.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Human Eye** (`#xywh=350,500,2219,1800`): Square framed watercolor illustration in the top-left corner depicting a single human eye with brown iris and eyelids, accompanying the section 'Augen schmerzen' (Eye Pains).

**Youth with Nosebleed** (`#xywh=350,2300,2219,1800`): Framed watercolor illustration in the bottom-left corner depicting a young man in profile wearing a yellow-brown garment, leaning forward with blood streaming from his nose into a small bowl held beneath his chin, accompanying the section 'Von Nasen. Fluetten' (Of Nosebleeds).

## Page 210 — IIIF Canvas #181

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0181.JP2` (5986 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0181.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0181.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 3,100

Continuation of remedies for dry tongue and mouth in fever (including psyllium/tragacanth mucilage, fresh butter/onion juice in rose vinegar, and houseleek with purified saltpetre/nitre), followed by a new section 'Von der Particular Angina in disen fieber' describing malignant/pestilential angina (quinsy) with erythema around the neck and chest, its rapid 24-hour to 7-day course, symptoms, and caution against bloodletting or purging except to protect the principal organs.

## Page 212 — IIIF Canvas #183

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0183.JP2` (5984 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0183.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0183.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,950 / 3,500

Two-column page containing two major sections: 'Von durch Brechen' (on diarrhoea/fluxes and vomiting caused by ingested poison or corrupted air/fruit, distinguishing fatal black/bloody fluxes from critical evacuations, followed by stomach-strengthening recipes using tormentil, ginger, cinnamon, paradise wood, mace, aristolochia, calamus, galangal, zedoary, and mastic) and 'Von Mißgeburdt in disem fieber' (on miscarriage and premature birth in fever, advising cautious purging and bloodletting between the 4th and 7th months using mild laxatives like manna and cassia).

## Page 213 — IIIF Canvas #184

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0184.JP2` (5718 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0184.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0184.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,800 / 2,300

Page 213 discusses dangerous blood fluxes and symptoms during malignant fevers in pregnant women, advising caution and moderation in bloodletting (phlebotomy) to preserve the fetus. It provides compound remedies, including a theriac electuary, external salves, and herbal decoctions (scolopendrium, hedera terrestris, ribwort plantain) to prevent miscarriage, as well as powders (white dittany, nigella, unicorn horn, hazel mistletoe) to aid difficult childbirth and expel a dead fetus or afterbirth. At the bottom, a new section begins under a red rubric concerning uterine flux (symbolized by ☿) in this fever.
